{"parentUuid":"451f8b44-68b9-471b-934d-1457359e348a","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a883719f9c59e0bc1","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. Triggers on \"generate an excalidraw image\", \"excalidraw-codex\", \"use codex for excalidraw\", or any request for excalidraw-style diagrams where no engine is named. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Sibling skill [[excalidraw-gemini]] does the same job through the paid Gemini API. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Triggers on any mention of LinkedIn, including \"write a LinkedIn post\", \"make a LinkedIn carousel\", \"swipeable LinkedIn post\", \"LinkedIn slides\", \"check my LinkedIn\", \"how did my posts do\", \"LinkedIn competitors\", \"post on LinkedIn\", \"browse LinkedIn feed\". Also triggers on \"check post engagement\", \"update post metrics\", or reviewing social media performance. Also use it for finding something to post about — \"find me a LinkedIn topic\", \"run a source sweep\", \"scout me something on MCP\", \"what should I post today\", or reviewing the drafts the daily scout left in Slack\n- newsletter-writer: Write personal newsletters in a conversational, essay-style format inspired by Ali Abdaal's style. Use when the user wants to write a newsletter issue, draft an email to their list, write a LifeNotes-style essay, or asks for help with newsletter writing. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. The analysis engine behind the acs/gap-scout routine, but also runs standalone. Use this skill WHENEVER the user pastes or links a coding/AI video (podcast, talk, tutorial, feature walkthrough) and wants the ACS angle: \"what ACS video could come from this\", \"run the gap scout on this\", \"gap-check this against the catalog\", \"find the spine idea\", \"is this already covered in the school\", \"what should I film from this\", \"mine this video for ACS\", or just drops a transcript/link and asks what's video-worthy. Also the per-video worker the gap-scout routine calls for each swept video. Do NOT use for a bulk channel sweep (that is the gap-scout routine / youtube-outlier-research routine) or for plain wisdom extraction with no ACS gap-check (that is extract-wisdom).\n- x-article\n- x-thread-miner\n- x-video-promo\n- youtube-ab-tester: End-to-end YouTube A/B testing for Ray's channel (@RAmjad) — title generation via subagent, thumbnail image generation via `gemini-3-pro-image-preview` at 1K, results recording, and the underlying scripts/golden references all live here. Use whenever the user wants to brainstorm titles, generate thumbnails (text or images), record A/B test results, analyze title/thumbnail performance, or asks things like \"suggest titles\", \"what titles should I test\", \"make a thumbnail\", \"thumbnail variations\", \"5 thumbnails for this video\", \"Matt style\", \"Nate style\", \"record these results\", \"what's working\", or \"update the A/B test results\". Also trigger when the user shares a screenshot of YouTube A/B test results and wants analysis or recording. Do NOT use for competitor title research (the youtube-outlier-research routine covers niche/competitor research) or video scripting (use youtube-scriptwriter instead).\n- youtube-miner\n- youtube-writer\n- review-plan\n- find-prompt-injections\n- loop-opportunities\n- loop-opportunities-personas\n- loop-opportunities-topdown\n- loopy-ai-class-review\n- loopy-ai-source-enrich\n- loopy-ai-stub-walk\n- mine-video-batch\n- claude-md-management:revise-claude-md\n- skill-creator:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- mermaid-diagram:mermaid-diagram-generator: Generate interactive HTML diagrams with Mermaid.js for visualizing flows, sequences, architectures, and processes. Use when the user asks to \"create a diagram\", \"visualize the flow\", \"draw a sequence diagram\", \"show me how X works\", or needs visual documentation of system interactions, workflows, database schemas, or technical processes.\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; Please try again.\");\n69\t        setAutoSigningIn(false);\n70\t      } else {\n71\t        router.push(data.redirect);\n72\t      }\n73\t    } catch {\n74\t      setLicenseError(\"An unexpected error occurred. License Key — enter HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX directly\n * 2. Email — magic link authentication via Better Auth\n *\n * Note: Any email can sign in. Admin features are shown based on\n * email being in the admin allowlist (checked in layout/middleware).\n */\nexport default function UserSignInPage() {\n  const router = useRouter();\n  const searchParams = useSearchParams();\n  const params = useParams();\n  const locale = (params.locale as string) || \"en\";\n\n  const [activeTab, setActiveTab] = useState<Tab>(\"license-key\");\n  const [autoSigningIn, setAutoSigningIn] = useState(false);\n\n  // License key tab state\n  const [licenseKey, setLicenseKey] = useState(\"\");\n  const [licenseSubmitting, setLicenseSubmitting] = useState(false);\n  const [licenseError, setLicenseError] = useState<string | null>(null);\n\n  // Email tab state\n  const [email, setEmail] = useState(\"\");\n  const [emailSubmitting, setEmailSubmitting] = useState(false);\n  const [magicLinkSent, setMagicLinkSent] = useState(false);\n  const [emailError, setEmailError] = useState<string | null>(null);\n\n  function getCallbackURL() {\n    const returnTo = searchParams.get(\"returnTo\");\n    return sanitizeReturnTo(returnTo, `/${locale}/user/dashboard`);\n  }\n\n  const licenseKeyParam = searchParams.get(\"licenseKey\");\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (!licenseKeyParam) return;\n    setLicenseKey(licenseKeyParam);\n    setAutoSigningIn(true);\n    handleLicenseKeySignInWithKey(licenseKeyParam);\n  }, []); // eslint-disable-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps\n\n  async function handleLicenseKeySignInWithKey(key: string) {\n    setLicenseError(null);\n    setLicenseSubmitting(true);\n    try {\n      const callbackURL = getCallbackURL();\n      const res = await fetch(\"/api/auth/sign-in/license-key\", {\n        method: \"POST\",\n        headers: { \"Content-Type\": \"application/json\" },\n        body: JSON.stringify({ licenseKey: key.trim(), callbackURL }),\n      });\n      const data = await res.json();\n      if (!res.ok) {\n        setLicenseError(data.error ?? \"Sign-in failed. Please try again.\");\n        setAutoSigningIn(false);\n      } else {\n        router.push(data.redirect);\n      }\n    } catch {\n      setLicenseError(\"An unexpected error occurred. Please try again.\");\n      setAutoSigningIn(false);\n    } finally {\n      setLicenseSubmitting(false);\n    }\n  }\n\n  async function handleLicenseKeySignIn(e?: React.FormEvent) {\n    e?.preventDefault();\n    await handleLicenseKeySignInWithKey(licenseKey);\n  }\n\n  async function handleSendMagicLink(e?: React.FormEvent) {\n    e?.preventDefault();\n    setEmailError(null);\n    setEmailSubmitting(true);\n\n    try {\n      const callbackURL = getCallbackURL();\n      const { error: authError } = await authClient.signIn.magicLink({\n        email,\n        callbackURL,\n      });\n\n      if (authError) {\n        setEmailError(authError.message ?? \"Failed to send magic link. Please try again.\");\n      } else {\n        setMagicLinkSent(true);\n      }\n    } catch {\n      setEmailError(\"An unexpected error occurred. Please try again.\");\n    } finally {\n      setEmailSubmitting(false);\n    }\n  }\n\n  const spinnerSvg = (\n    <svg className=\"animate-spin h-5 w-5\" fill=\"none\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\">\n      <circle className=\"opacity-25\" cx=\"12\" cy=\"12\" r=\"10\" stroke=\"currentColor\" strokeWidth=\"4\" />\n      <path className=\"opacity-75\" fill=\"currentColor\" d=\"M4 12a8 8 0 018-8V0C5.373 0 0 5.373 0 12h4zm2 5.291A7.962 7.962 0 014 12H0c0 3.042 1.135 5.824 3 7.938l3-2.647z\" />\n    </svg>\n  );\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"min-h-screen flex items-center justify-center bg-gradient-to-br from-slate-900 via-slate-800 to-slate-900 py-12 px-4\">\n      <div className=\"max-w-md w-full\">\n        <div className=\"bg-white/10 backdrop-blur-lg rounded-2xl p-8 shadow-2xl border border-white/20\">\n          {/* Header */}\n          <div className=\"text-center mb-8\">\n            <h1 className=\"text-2xl font-bold text-white mb-2\">HyperWhisper</h1>\n            <p className=\"text-gray-400 text-sm\">\n              Sign in to view your licenses and credits\n            </p>\n          </div>\n\n          {/* Auto sign-in loading overlay */}\n          {autoSigningIn ? (\n            <div className=\"flex flex-col items-center justify-center py-8 gap-4\">\n              <svg className=\"animate-spin h-8 w-8 text-emerald-400\" fill=\"none\" viewBox=\"0 0 24 24\">\n 12a8 8 0 018-8V0C5.373 0 0 5.373 0 12h4zm2 5.291A7.962 7.962 0 014 12H0c0 3.042 1.135 5.824 3 7.938l3-2.647z\" />\n              </svg>\n              <p className=\"text-gray-300 text-sm\">Signing you in…</p>\n            </div>\n          ) : (\n          <>\n\n          {/* Tab Switcher */}\n          <div className=\"flex rounded-lg bg-white/5 border border-white/10 p-1 mb-6\">\n            <button\n              type=\"button\"\n              onClick={() => setActiveTab(\"license-key\")}\n              className={`flex-1 py-2 px-3 rounded-md text-sm font-medium transition-all duration-200 ${\n                activeTab === \"license-key\"\n                  ? \"bg-white/15 text-white shadow-sm\"\n                  : \"text-gray-400 hover:text-gray-300\"\n              }`}\n className={`flex-1 py-2 px-3 rounded-md text-sm font-medium transition-all duration-200 ${\n                activeTab === \"email\"\n                  ? \"bg-white/15 text-white shadow-sm\"\n                  : \"text-gray-400 hover:text-gray-300\"\n              }`}\n            >\n              Email\n            </button>\n          </div>\n\n          {/* License Key Tab */}\n          {activeTab === \"license-key\" && (\n            <form onSubmit={handleLicenseKeySignIn} className=\"space-y-6\">\n              {licenseError && (\n                <div className=\"p-4 bg-red-500/20 border border-red-500/30 rounded-lg\">\n                  <p className=\"text-red-300 text-sm text-center\">{licenseError}</p>\n                </div>\n              )}\n\n              <div>\n (\n                <form onSubmit={handleSendMagicLink} className=\"space-y-6\">\n                  {emailError && (\n                    <div className=\"p-4 bg-red-500/20 border border-red-500/30 rounded-lg\">\n                      <p className=\"text-red-300 text-sm text-center\">{emailError}</p>\n                    </div>\n                  )}\n\n                  <div>\n                    <label htmlFor=\"email\" className=\"block text-sm font-medium text-gray-300 mb-2\">\n                      Email address\n                    </label>\n                    <input\n                      id=\"email\"\n                      type=\"email\"\n                      required\n                      value={email}\n                      onChange={(e) => setEmail(e.target.value)}\n                      className=\"w-full px-4 py-3 bg-white/5 border border-white/10 rounded-lg text-white placeholder-gray-500 focus:outline-none focus:ring-2 focus:ring-emerald-500 focus:border-transparent transition-all\"\n                      placeholder=\"your@email.com\"\n                    />\n                    <p className=\"text-gray-500 text-xs mt-2\">\n                      Use the same email you used to purchase your license\n                    </p>\n                  </div>\n\n                  <div className=\"space-y-3\">\n                    <button\n                      type=\"submit\"\n                      disabled={emailSubmitting}\n                      className=\"w-full py-3 px-4 bg-gradient-to-r from-emerald-500 to-teal-500 text-white font-semibold rounded-lg hover:from-emerald-600 hover:to-teal-600 transition-all duration-200 shadow-lg hover:shadow-xl disabled:opacity-50 disabled:cursor-not-allowed\"\n                    >\n                      {emailSubmitting ? {"parentUuid":"7fb7c7dc-7bd2-4123-a501-d85f266a0a8d","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"e61e834d-815b-4b42-a7af-2d703ec1db4b","agentId":"a883719f9c59e0bc1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01EYzyu9RogWHoJ7zBoDFQhb","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n2\t\n3\timport CreditsPurchase from \"@/components/credits/CreditsPurchase\";\n4\t\n5\t/**\n6\t * Credits Page\n7\t *\n8\t * Default (no params): the guest buy-credits flow — enter an email, pick an\n9\t * amount, and check out. The webhook mints a license key (the wallet) and emails\n10\t * it. See components/credits/CreditsPurchase.tsx.\n11\t *\n12\t * Native-app deep links still carry a key and route through sign-in so the user\n13\t * lands on their dashboard to top up an existing wallet:\n14\t *   - ?license_key=HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX (macOS, Windows)\n15\t *   - ?id=HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX          (legacy macOS — kept for older app versions)\n16\t */\n17\texport default async function CreditsPage({\n18\t  params,\n19\t  searchParams,\n20\t}: {\n21\t  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n22\t  searchParams: Promise<{ id?: string; license_key?: string }>;\n23\t}) {\n24\t  const { locale } = await params;\n25\t  const { id, license_key } = await searchParams;\n26\t  const raw = id ?? license_key ?? \"\";\n27\t\n28\t  if (raw.startsWith(\"HW-\")) {\n29\t    redirect(\n30\t      `/${locale}/user/sign-in?licenseKey=${encodeURIComponent(raw)}&returnTo=/${locale}/user/dashboard`\n31\t    );\n32\t  }\n33\t\n34\t  return (\n35\t    <main className=\"min-h-screen bg-black px-6 py-24 md:py-28\">\n36\t      <CreditsPurchase locale={locale} />\n37\t    </main>\n38\t  );\n39\t}\n40\t"}]},"uuid":"b74fe39b-aa71-45f9-8fd2-c544c7941c73","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:34:42.213Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/app/[locale]/credits/page.tsx","content":"import { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n\nimport CreditsPurchase from \"@/components/credits/CreditsPurchase\";\n\n/**\n * Credits Page\n *\n * Default (no params): the guest buy-credits flow — enter an email, pick an\n * amount, and check out. The webhook mints a license key (the wallet) and emails\n * it. See components/credits/CreditsPurchase.tsx.\n *\n * Native-app deep links still carry a key and route through sign-in so the user\n * lands on their dashboard to top up an existing wallet:\n *   - ?license_key=HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX (macOS, Windows)\n *   - ?id=HW-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX          (legacy macOS — kept for older app versions)\n */\nexport default async function CreditsPage({\n  params,\n  searchParams,\n}: {\n  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n  searchParams: Promise<{ id?: string; license_key?: string }>;\n}) {\n  const { locale } = await params;\n  const { id, license_key } = await searchParams;\n  const raw = id ?? license_key ?? \"\";\n\n  if (raw.startsWith(\"HW-\")) {\n    redirect(\n      `/${locale}/user/sign-in?licenseKey=${encodeURIComponent(raw)}&returnTo=/${locale}/user/dashboard`\n    );\n  }\n\n  return (\n    <main className=\"min-h-screen bg-black px-6 py-24 md:py-28\">\n      <CreditsPurchase locale={locale} />\n    </main>\n  );\n}\n","numLines":40,"startLine":1,"totalLines":40}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7fb7c7dc-7bd2-4123-a501-d85f266a0a8d","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"e5024391-980f-46c1-9db3-9424d1e97071","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"e61e834d-815b-4b42-a7af-2d703ec1db4b","agentId":"a883719f9c59e0bc1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01LBfQqkGvwM34DdJMuFu35G","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"use client\";\n2\t\n3\timport { usePathname } from \"next/navigation\";\n4\timport { useEffect, useState } from \"react\";\n5\t\n6\timport { Navbar } from \"@/components/navbar\";\n7\timport FooterSection from \"@/components/landing/FooterSection\";\n8\timport { OpenSourceBanner } from \"@/components/open-source-banner\";\n9\t\n10\t/**\n11\t * Client-side layout wrapper that conditionally renders navbar/footer\n12\t * based on the current pathname.\n13\t *\n14\t * Full-screen routes (no navbar/footer):\n15\t * - /user/*\n16\t */\n17\texport default function LayoutWrapper({\n18\t  children,\n19\t}: {\n20\t  children: React.ReactNode;\n21\t}) {\n22\t  const pathname = usePathname();\n23\t\n24\t  // Open-source announcement banner: shown until the user dismisses it.\n25\t  // Defaults to visible so SSR and first client render match; hidden after\n26\t  // mount if a prior dismissal is stored.\n27\t  const [bannerDismissed, setBannerDismissed] = useState(false);\n28\t\n29\t  useEffect(() => {\n30\t    if (localStorage.getItem(\"os-banner-dismissed\") === \"1\") {\n31\t      setBannerDismissed(true);\n32\t    }\n33\t  }, []);\n34\t\n35\t  const dismissBanner = () => {\n36\t    setBannerDismissed(true);\n37\t    localStorage.setItem(\"os-banner-dismissed\", \"1\");\n38\t  };\n39\t\n40\t  // Check if this is a full-screen route (user portal)\n41\t  const isFullScreenRoute = pathname.includes(\"/user\");\n42\t\n43\t  if (isFullScreenRoute) {\n44\t    // Full-screen layout: no navbar/footer\n45\t    return <div className=\"min-h-screen\">{children}</div>;\n46\t  }\n47\t\n48\t  const showBanner = !bannerDismissed;\n49\t\n50\t  // Regular layout: with navbar and footer\n51\t  return (\n52\t    <div className=\"relative flex flex-col min-h-screen\">\n53\t      {/*\n54\t        Sticky header holds the announcement banner + navbar. Using `sticky`\n55\t        (not `fixed`) keeps it in normal document flow, so the banner naturally\n56\t        pushes the navbar and page content down — no manual spacer / height math\n57\t        to keep in sync, which previously caused the header to be cut off.\n58\t      */}\n59\t      <div className=\"sticky top-0 z-50 w-full flex flex-col\">\n60\t        {showBanner && <OpenSourceBanner onDismiss={dismissBanner} />}\n61\t        <Navbar />\n62\t      </div>\n63\t      <main className=\"container mx-auto max-w-7xl px-6 flex-grow\">\n64\t        {children}\n65\t      </main>\n66\t      <FooterSection />\n67\t    </div>\n68\t  );\n69\t}\n70\t"}]},"uuid":"2dbad66a-0474-4824-b828-7384b71d2f31","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:34:42.388Z","toolUseResult":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/components/layout/LayoutWrapper.tsx","content":"\"use client\";\n\nimport { usePathname } from \"next/navigation\";\nimport { useEffect, useState } from \"react\";\n\nimport { Navbar } from \"@/components/navbar\";\nimport FooterSection from \"@/components/landing/FooterSection\";\nimport { OpenSourceBanner } from \"@/components/open-source-banner\";\n\n/**\n * Client-side layout wrapper that conditionally renders navbar/footer\n * based on the current pathname.\n *\n * Full-screen routes (no navbar/footer):\n * - /user/*\n */\nexport default function LayoutWrapper({\n  children,\n}: {\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n}) {\n  const pathname = usePathname();\n\n  // Open-source announcement banner: shown until the user dismisses it.\n  // Defaults to visible so SSR and first client render match; hidden after\n  // mount if a prior dismissal is stored.\n  const [bannerDismissed, setBannerDismissed] = useState(false);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (localStorage.getItem(\"os-banner-dismissed\") === \"1\") {\n      setBannerDismissed(true);\n    }\n  }, []);\n\n  const dismissBanner = () => {\n    setBannerDismissed(true);\n    localStorage.setItem(\"os-banner-dismissed\", \"1\");\n  };\n\n  // Check if this is a full-screen route (user portal)\n  const isFullScreenRoute = pathname.includes(\"/user\");\n\n  if (isFullScreenRoute) {\n    // Full-screen layout: no navbar/footer\n    return <div className=\"min-h-screen\">{children}</div>;\n  }\n\n  const showBanner = !bannerDismissed;\n\n  // Regular layout: with navbar and footer\n  return (\n    <div className=\"relative flex flex-col min-h-screen\">\n      {/*\n        Sticky header holds the announcement banner + navbar. Using `sticky`\n        (not `fixed`) keeps it in normal document flow, so the banner naturally\n        pushes the navbar and page content down — no manual spacer / height math\n        to keep in sync, which previously caused the header to be cut off.\n      */}\n      <div className=\"sticky top-0 z-50 w-full flex flex-col\">\n        {showBanner && <OpenSourceBanner onDismiss={dismissBanner} />}\n        <Navbar />\n      </div>\n      <main className=\"container mx-auto max-w-7xl px-6 flex-grow\">\n        {children}\n      </main>\n      <FooterSection />\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n","numLines":70,"startLine":1,"totalLines":70}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e5024391-980f-46c1-9db3-9424d1e97071","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

Rejects protocol-relative (\"//evil.com\") and\n * backslash (\"/\\\\evil.com\") prefixes that satisfy a naive `startsWith(\"/\")`\n * check but resolve to an attacker-controlled origin, preventing open redirects.\n */\nexport function sanitizeReturnTo(\n  returnTo: string | null | undefined,\n  fallback: string,\n) {\n  if (\n    returnTo &&\n    returnTo.startsWith(\"/\") &&\n    !returnTo.startsWith(\"//\") &&\n    !returnTo.startsWith(\"/\\\\\")\n  ) {\n    return returnTo;\n  }\n\n  return fallback;\n}\n\nexport function sanitizeLicenseKeyRedirect(callbackURL: string | \"@/contexts/DownloadModalContext\";\nimport DownloadModal from \"@/components/landing/DownloadModal\";\nimport { PostHogClientProvider } from \"@/contexts/PostHogProvider\";\n\nexport interface ProvidersProps {\n  children: React.ReactNode;\n  themeProps?: ThemeProviderProps;\n}\n\ndeclare module \"@react-types/shared\" {\n  interface RouterConfig {\n    routerOptions: NonNullable<\n      Parameters<ReturnType<typeof useRouter>[\"push\"]>[1]\n    >;\n  }\n}\n\nexport function Providers({ children, themeProps }: ProvidersProps) {\n  const router = useRouter();\n\n  return (\n    <TRPCProvider>\n      <HeroUIProvider navigate={router.push}>\n        <PostHogClientProvider>\n          <NextThemesProvider {...themeProps}>\n            <LazyMotion features={domAnimation}>\n              <DownloadModalProvider>\n                {children}\n                <DownloadModal />\n              </DownloadModalProvider>\n            </LazyMotion>\n          </NextThemesProvider>\n null;\r\n  } catch {\r\n    return null;\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * Middleware that handles:\r\n * 1. next-intl locale routing (adds locale prefix)\r\n * 2. { createAuthEndpoint } from \"better-auth/api\";\nimport { setSessionCookie } from \"better-auth/cookies\";\nimport { eq } from \"drizzle-orm\";\nimport { z } from \"zod\";\n\nimport { findAccountByKey } from \"./db-layer\";\nimport { sanitizeLicenseKeyRedirect } from \"./license-key-redirect\";\n\nimport { db } from \"@/src/db\";\nimport { user } from \"@/src/db/schema/auth\";\n\nexport const licenseKeyPlugin = () => ({\n  id: \"license-key\",\n  endpoints: {\n    signInLicenseKey: createAuthEndpoint(\n      \"/sign-in/license-key\",\n      {\n        method: \"POST\",\n        requireHeaders: true,\n        body: z.object({\n          licenseKey: z.string(),\n          callbackURL: z.string().optional(),\n        }),\n      },\n      async (ctx) => {\n        const { licenseKey, callbackURL } = ctx.body;\n\n        const license = await findAccountByKey(licenseKey);\n\n        if (!license || license.status !== \"granted\") {\n          return ctx.json(\n            { error: \"Invalid or inactive license key.\" },\n            { status: 400 },\n          );\n        }\n\n        if (!license.userId) {\n          return ctx.json(\n            {\n              error:\n                \"No account found for this license key. Please contact support.\",\n            },\n            { status: 400 },\n          );\n        }\n\n        const [foundUser] = await db\n          .select()\n          .from(user)\n          .where(eq(user.id, license.userId))\n          .limit(1);\n\n        if (!foundUser) {\n          return ctx.json(\n            {\n              error:\n                \"No account found for this license key. Please contact support.\",\n            },\n            { status: 400 },\n          );\n        }\n\n        const session = await ctx.context.internalAdapter.createSession(\n          foundUser.id,\n        );\n\n        if (!session) {\n          return ctx.json(\n            { error: \"Failed to create session.\" },\n            { status: 500 },\n          );\n        }\n\n        await setSessionCookie(ctx, { session, user: foundUser });\n\n        const ipAddress =\n          ctx.headers?.get(\"x-forwarded-for\")?.split(\",\")[0]?.trim() ??\n          \"unknown\";\n        const userAgent = ctx.headers?.get(\"user-agent\") ?? \"unknown\";\n        console.log(\n          `[license-key-sign-in] user=${foundUser.id} license=${license.id} ip=${ipAddress} ua=\"${userAgent}\"`,\n        );\n\n        return ctx.json({ redirect: sanitizeLicenseKeyRedirect(callbackURL) });\n      },\n    ),\n  },\n  rateLimit: [\n    {\n      pathMatcher(path: string) {\n        return path === \"/sign-in/license-key\";\n      },\n      window: 60,\n      max: 5,\n    },\n  ],\n});\n","numLines":98,"startLine":1,"totalLines":98}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"ecc7140d-ba05-479c-b507-11b7415fa8da","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"49196d8f-7730-448d-8d6f-ecdc38dfffd5","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"7755d08f-e409-4455-9246-4ac14dedbb5f","agentId":"a883719f9c59e0bc1","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WBiPFbLshBAhTjd62y8rZi","type":"tool_result","content":"=== router.replace / pushState / searchParams mutation ===\ncomponents/language-switcher.tsx:28:      router.replace(pathname, { locale: newLocale });\r\n=== checkout page ===\nimport { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n\n/**\n * /checkout — legacy purchase entry point (kept as a redirect).\n *\n * The standalone license checkout was retired: buying credits now mints the key\n * (the license IS the wallet). Native apps (macOS `LicenseManager.openPurchasePage`,\n * Windows `PurchaseUrl`) and the docs still link to `/checkout`, so this page\n * stays as a permanent redirect into the credits buy flow. A license key (if\n * present) is forwarded so existing users land on the top-up path rather than\n * minting a second key; a `code` (promo) param is preserved too.\n */\nexport default async function CheckoutPage({\n  params,\n  searchParams,\n}: {\n  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n  searchParams: Promise<{ id?: string; license_key?: string; code?: string }>;\n}) {\n  const { locale } = await params;\n  const { id, license_key, code } = await searchParams;\n\n  const query = new URLSearchParams();\n  const key = id ?? license_key;\n  if (key) query.set(\"license_key\", key);\n  if (code) query.set(\"code\", code);\n  const qs = query.toString();\n\n  redirect(`/${locale}/credits${qs ? `?${qs}` : \"\"}`);\n}","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"fa4bfc2d-e7fc-43fd-a96e-51a04ddfece0","timestamp":"2026-07-29T11:38:40.729Z","toolUseResult":{"stdout":"=== router.replace / pushState / searchParams mutation ===\ncomponents/language-switcher.tsx:28:      router.replace(pathname, { locale: newLocale });\r\n=== checkout page ===\nimport { redirect } from \"next/navigation\";\n\n/**\n * /checkout — legacy purchase entry point (kept as a redirect).\n *\n * The standalone license checkout was retired: buying credits now mints the key\n * (the license IS the wallet). Native apps (macOS `LicenseManager.openPurchasePage`,\n * Windows `PurchaseUrl`) and the docs still link to `/checkout`, so this page\n * stays as a permanent redirect into the credits buy flow. A license key (if\n * present) is forwarded so existing users land on the top-up path rather than\n * minting a second key; a `code` (promo) param is preserved too.\n */\nexport default async function CheckoutPage({\n  params,\n  searchParams,\n}: {\n  params: Promise<{ locale: string }>;\n  searchParams: Promise<{ id?: string; license_key?: string; code?: string }>;\n}) {\n  const { locale } = await params;\n  const { id, license_key, code } = await searchParams;\n\n  const query = new URLSearchParams();\n  const key = id ?? license_key;\n  if (key) query.set(\"license_key\", key);\n  if (code) query.set(\"code\", code);\n  const qs = query.toString();\n\n  redirect(`/${locale}/credits${qs ? `?${qs}` : \"\"}`);\n}","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"49196d8f-7730-448d-8d6f-ecdc38dfffd5","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"821f0377-c0df-43ce-8182-a92bcf9df5a5","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a883719f9c59e0bc1","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdWHPjLDirkdDq2TXGhPt","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Both leads resolve. Here is what the code actually shows.\n\n**BENIGN [A6]** — `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/sign-in/page.tsx:54`. The mount-only effect can only misbehave if `licenseKeyParam` transitions null → non-null while the component stays mounted, and no such path exists. The sole producer of `?licenseKey=` is a *server* `redirect()` from a different route segment — `nextjs/app/[locale]/credits/page.tsx:29-31`, `` redirect(`/${locale}/user/sign-in?licenseKey=${encodeURIComponent(raw)}&returnTo=...`) `` (fed by the native deep links `?license_key=`/`?id=`, and by `nextjs/app/[locale]/checkout/page.tsx:29` which forwards into the same page) — so the sign-in page always mounts fresh with the param already present. Nothing can mutate the query in place while sign-in is mounted: the only in-place URL mutation in the whole app is `nextjs/components/language-switcher.tsx:28` `router.replace(pathname, { locale: newLocale })`, which lives in the Navbar, and `nextjs/components/layout/LayoutWrapper.tsx:41-46` renders no Navbar on `/user/*` (`const isFullScreenRoute = pathname.includes(\"/user\")` → bare `<div className=\"min-h-screen\">{children}</div>`). The page's own two navigations are `router.push(\\`/${locale}\\`)` (unmounts) and `router.push(data.redirect)` to a sanitized non-sign-in path. The stale-closure sub-shapes are also dead: `getCallbackURL()` and `locale` are captured at mount, which is exactly when the URL carries the correct values, and there is no session/context object in this component at all (`authClient` is only used for `signIn.magicLink`; the \"already signed in\" check lives server-side in `nextjs/middleware.ts:126-133`, not in this effect). I also confirmed there is no prerender window that would hand the effect empty search params: `/[locale]/user/sign-in` is absent from `.next/prerender-manifest.json` (40 prerendered routes, none matching), because `headers()` in the locale layout's `generateMetadata` forces dynamic rendering — so SSR sees the real query string. The dep array is still wrong as written (`[licenseKeyParam]` is the honest form); it is latent, not reachable.\n\n**BENIGN [A7]** — `nextjs/app/[locale]/layout.tsx:7`. The stub has **no backing store**, so the cross-request-leak shape is structurally impossible: `{ getItem: () => null, setItem: noop, removeItem: noop, clear: noop, key: () => null, length: 0 }` — every write is `const noop = () => {}` and every read is a constant `null`. Nothing user-specific can ever be held on `global`. The browser-misdetection shape is also dead: `grep -rl \"typeof localStorage\" node_modules/` returns **zero** hits, and both dependencies that touch bare (unprefixed) `localStorage` during server render gate on `window`, not on `localStorage` — `next-themes` (`var O = typeof window == \"undefined\"` … `H = (e,i) => { if (O) return; … localStorage.getItem(e) … }`) and better-auth's broadcast channel (`post(message) { if (typeof window === \"undefined\") return; try { localStorage.setItem(...) } catch {} }`). \"./auth-license-key-plugin\";\n\nexport const auth = betterAuth({\n  baseURL: process.env.BETTER_AUTH_URL,\n  database: drizzleAdapter(db, { provider: \"pg\" }),\n  user: {\n    additionalFields: {\n      role: {\n        type: \"string\",\n        required: false,\n        defaultValue: \"user\",\n        input: false,\n      },\n    },\n  },\n  plugins: [\n    magicLink({\n      sendMagicLink: async ({ email, url }) => {\n        await resend.emails.send({\n          from: DEFAULT_FROM_EMAIL,\n          to: email,\n          subject: \"Sign in to HyperWhisper\",\n          html: magicLinkEmailHtml({ url }),\n        });\n      },\n    }),\n    licenseKeyPlugin(),\n    nextCookies(),\n  ],\n});\n\nfunction magicLinkEmailHtml({ url }: { url: string }): string {\n  return `\n<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html>\n<head>\n  <meta charset=\"utf-8\">\n  <meta name=\"viewport\" content=\"width=device-width, initial-scale=1.0\">\n</head>\n<body Both BENIGN. Blocks verbatim at the end. First, your extra question — I resolved it decisively, and the answer redirects your open-redirect to a different file.\n\nANSWER: better-auth DOES validate and reject the tab/CR/LF callbackURL, on BOTH the magic-link and license-key endpoints. So the `sanitizeReturnTo` gap does NOT become an off-origin callback delivery on either sign-in path.\n\nMechanism, with the code:\n1. `node_modules/better-auth/dist/api/index.mjs:157-160` registers the check as a router-level middleware on every path — `routerMiddleware: [{ path: \"/**\", middleware: originCheckMiddleware }, ...middlewares]`. `pluginEndpoints` are spread into the same `api` object, so your custom `/sign-in/license-key` endpoint (`nextjs/src/lib/auth-license-key-plugin.ts`) is covered too, even though it declares no `use: [originCheck(...)]` of its own.\n2. `origin-check.mjs` pulls `const callbackURL = body?.callbackURL || query?.callbackURL` and runs `ctx.context.isTrustedOrigin(url, { allowRelativePaths: true })`, throwing `INVALID_CALLBACK_URL` (403) on failure.\n3. The relative-path validator is `trusted-origins.mjs` `matchesOriginPattern`: `/^\\/(?!\\/|\\\\|%2f|%5c)[\\w\\-.\\+/@]*(?:\\?[\\w\\-.\\+/=&%@]*)?$/`. The path character class `[\\w\\-.\\+/@]` excludes tab, CR, LF and `%`, so it is an allowlist, not a blocklist like `sanitizeReturnTo`.\n\nI ran the regex against the payloads (read-only, no network):\n  \"/en/user/dashboard\" -> true\n  \"/\\t/evil.com\" -> false     \"/\\n/evil.com\" -> false     \"/\\r/evil.com\" -> false\n  \"/%09/evil.com\" -> false    \"//evil.com\" -> false\nSo a tainted `getCallbackURL()` gets a 403 before the license-key handler ever runs, which also means `{ redirect: sanitizeLicenseKeyRedirect(callbackURL) }` never returns a tainted value to `router.push(data.redirect)` at page.tsx:71. `NO trustedOrigins in nextjs/src/lib/auth.ts` is not load-bearing here — the relative-path branch returns before `pattern` is consulted at all.\n\nWHERE IT DOES LAND — reassign this, it is outside my lead: `nextjs/middleware.ts:126-133`. The already-signed-in branch never touches better-auth:\n\n    if (hasSessionCookie(request)) {\n      const returnTo = request.nextUrl.searchParams.get(\"returnTo\");\n      const redirectUrl = sanitizeReturnTo(returnTo, `/${locale}/user/dashboard`);\n      return NextResponse.redirect(new URL(redirectUrl, request.url));\n    }\n\nI verified the full chain end to end:\n  `new URL(\"https://hyperwhisper.com/en/user/sign-in?returnTo=/%09/evil.com\").searchParams.get(\"returnTo\")` percent-decodes to the literal `\"/\\t/evil.com\"` -> repo `sanitizeReturnTo` accepts it (starts with \"/\", not \"//\", not \"/\\\\\") -> `new URL(rt, request.url).href` === `https://evil.com/`.\nThe WHATWG parser strips the tab, collapsing it to protocol-relative. Same for `%0a`/`%0d`. Note the decode step is load-bearing: the raw string `/%09/evil.com` does NOT collapse (`https://hyperwhisper.com/%09/evil.com`), it only collapses after `searchParams.get` decodes it — so the entry point must be the query param, which it is. Trigger: any authenticated user is sent to `https://evil.com` by a link to `/en/user/sign-in?returnTo=/%09/evil.com`, and `middleware.ts:93` + `:120` are the other two `sanitizeReturnTo` consumers worth checking. `hasSessionCookie` gating makes it an authenticated open redirect, phishing-grade, not a nothing.\n\nMY BLOCKS, unchanged by the above (the open redirect is in middleware.ts, not at either oracle line, and is not caused by the dep array or the polyfill):\n\nBENIGN: [A6] `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/sign-in/page.tsx:54` — the mount-only effect can only misbehave if `licenseKeyParam` goes null -> non-null while mounted, and no such path exists: the sole producer of `?licenseKey=` is a server `redirect()` from a different route segment (`app/[locale]/credits/page.tsx:29-31`, fed by the native deep links and by `checkout/page.tsx:29`), so sign-in always mounts fresh with the param present; nothing can mutate the query in place, since the only in-place URL mutation in the app is `components/language-switcher.tsx:28` `router.replace(pathname, { locale: newLocale })` in the Navbar, and `components/layout/LayoutWrapper.tsx:41-46` renders no Navbar on `/user/*` (`const isFullScreenRoute = pathname.includes(\"/user\")` -> bare `<div className=\"min-h-screen\">{children}</div>`); the stale-closure sub-shapes are dead because `getCallbackURL()` and `locale` are captured at mount when the URL is already correct and no session/context object exists in this component (the \"already signed in\" check lives in `middleware.ts:126-133`); and there is no prerender window handing the effect empty params, since `/[locale]/user/sign-in` is absent from `.next/prerender-manifest.json` (40 prerendered routes, none matching) because `headers()` in the locale layout's `generateMetadata` forces dynamic rendering. `[licenseKeyParam]` is still the honest dep array — latent, not reachable.\n\nBENIGN: [A7] `nextjs/app/[locale]/layout.tsx:7` — the cross-request leak is structurally impossible because the stub has no backing store, `{ getItem: () => null, setItem: noop, removeItem: noop, clear: noop, key: () => null, length: 0 }` with `const noop = () => {}`, so nothing user-specific can ever be held on `global`; the browser-misdetection shape is dead because `grep -rl \"typeof localStorage\" node_modules/` returns zero hits and both deps that touch bare `localStorage` during server render gate on `window`, not on `localStorage` — next-themes (`var O = typeof window == \"undefined\"` ... `H = (e,i) => { if (O) return; ... localStorage.getItem(e) ... }`) and better-auth's broadcast channel (`post(message) { if (typeof window === \"undefined\") return; try { localStorage.setItem(...) } catch {} }`), while PostHog reaches it as `d.localStorage` off `window` guarded by `null == d`. Two secondary notes: the polyfill is dead code (the only in-repo `localStorage` calls, `LayoutWrapper.tsx:30` and `:37`, are inside a `useEffect` and a click handler, both client-only), and it is mis-ordered — ESM import hoisting runs it after every import in the file, confirmed in the compiled output `.next/server/chunks/5630.js` which emits all `c(52974)...c(4042)` requires before `{let a=()=>{};global.localStorage={...}}` — so it could not have protected any module-init-time access it was written to guard.\n\nBENIGN: [signal 3] `nextjs/app/[locale]/error.tsx:14` — `/* eslint-disable no-console */` above `console.error(error)` in an error boundary. Correct use.\n\nNo CANDIDATE findings in this cluster.","type":"message","recipient":"oracle-hunter","content":"Lead [A6/A7] complete. Both BENIGN. Final report:\n\n## Lead [A6/A7] — both BENIGN, no candidates\n\n**BENIGN: [A6]** `/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/app/[locale]/user/sign-in/page.tsx:54` — the mount-only effect can only misbehave if `licenseKeyParam` goes null → non-null while mounted, and no such path exists. The sole producer of `?licenseKey=` is a *server* `redirect()` from a different route segment (`app/[locale]/credits/page.tsx:29-31`, fed by the native deep links and by `checkout/page.tsx:29`), so sign-in always mounts fresh with the param present. Nothing can mutate the query in place: the only in-place URL mutation in the app is `components/language-switcher.tsx:28` `router.replace(pathname, { locale: newLocale })`, which lives in the Navbar, and `components/layout/LayoutWrapper.tsx:41-46` renders no Navbar on `/user/*` (`const isFullScreenRoute = pathname.includes(\"/user\")` → bare `<div className=\"min-h-screen\">{children}</div>`). Stale-closure sub-shapes are dead — `getCallbackURL()` and `locale` are captured at mount when the URL is already correct, and no session/context object exists in this component (the \"already signed in\" check lives in `middleware.ts:126-133`). No prerender window either: `/[locale]/user/sign-in` is absent from `.next/prerender-manifest.json` (40 prerendered routes, none matching), because `headers()` in the locale layout's `generateMetadata` forces dynamic rendering.\n\n**BENIGN: [A7]** `/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/app/[locale]/layout.tsx:7` — the cross-request leak is structurally impossible: the stub has **no backing store** (`getItem: () => null`, every write is `const noop = () => {}`). The browser-misdetection shape is dead too — `grep -rl \"typeof localStorage\" node_modules/` returns zero hits, and both deps touching bare `localStorage` during server render gate on `window`, not `localStorage`. Also dead code (the only in-repo uses, `LayoutWrapper.tsx:30`/`:37`, are client-only) and mis-ordered — ESM hoisting runs it after every import, confirmed in `.next/server/chunks/5630.js`.\n\n**BENIGN: [signal 3]** `app/[locale]/error.tsx:14` — correct use in an error boundary.\n\n## Answer to the lead's follow-up (changes their attribution)\n\n**better-auth DOES block the tainted `callbackURL`**, on both the magic-link and license-key endpoints — `api/index.mjs:157-160` registers `originCheckMiddleware` on `path: \"/**\"`, covering custom plugin endpoints, and `trusted-origins.mjs` validates relative paths with an **allowlist** regex whose character class `[\\w\\-.\\+/@]` excludes tab/CR/LF/`%`. Verified: `/\\t/evil.com`, `/\\n/evil.com`, `/\\r/evil.com`, `/%09/evil.com`, `//evil.com` all → `false` → 403. The missing `trustedOrigins` in `auth.ts` is not load-bearing (the relative-path branch returns before `pattern` is consulted).\n\n**But the gap does land somewhere real, outside my lead:** `/Users/ray/Desktop/hyperwhisper-public/nextjs/middleware.ts:126-133`, the already-signed-in branch, never touches better-auth.
